Well, I came in because I’ve just been feeling like my head isn’t working the way it should. It’s like there’s a fog over everything, and I keep mixing up simple things that I normally would have no trouble keeping straight. 

For example, I’ll get appointment times wrong, or I’ll think something is happening on one day when it’s another. I’ve been confused about exhibit dates too, and then I second-guess myself and have to check everything again. And I volunteer—well, I try to keep track of who I’ve called and who I haven’t—and lately I’ll realize I’m not sure whether I already called someone or just meant to call them. It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.

Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.

I wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.

And I volunteer—well, I try to keep track of who I’ve called and who I haven’t—and lately I’ll realize I’m not sure whether I already called someone or just meant to call them. It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad exactly. I mean, I’ve been frustrated and worried because I don’t like feeling so mixed up, and it gets to me when I make mistakes with things I used to handle just fine. But “sad or down”... I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 79 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Loss_of_Interest\nmanifestation: \"Things I used to love, like my watercolors, my tomato plants, line dancing, and watching the cardinals out back, just sit there now and I can't make myself care about them.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.

I used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.

And I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.

Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.

It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad exactly. I mean, I’ve been frustrated and worried because I don’t like feeling so mixed up, and it gets to me when I make mistakes with things I used to handle just fine. But “sad or down”... I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.

I haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.

I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.

Like my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.

And line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.

So yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.

It’s like there’s a fog over everything, and I keep mixing up simple things that I normally would have no trouble keeping straight. \n\nFor example, I’ll get appointment times wrong, or I’ll think something is happening on one day when it’s another. I’ve been confused about exhibit dates too, and then I second-guess myself and have to check everything again. And I volunteer—well, I try to keep track of who I’ve called and who I haven’t—and lately I’ll realize I’m not sure whether I already called someone or just meant to call them. It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad exactly. I mean, I’ve been frustrated and worried because I don’t like feeling so mixed up, and it gets to me when I make mistakes with things I used to handle just fine. But “sad or down”... I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.

I do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.

So… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.

From the dialogue so far and your current candidates, ask the one follow-up question that best separates them.\n\n[Current Turn]\n9\n\n[Current Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F43.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I’ve just been feeling like my head isn’t working the way it should. It’s like there’s a fog over everything, and I keep mixing up simple things that I normally would have no trouble keeping straight. \n\nFor example, I’ll get appointment times wrong, or I’ll think something is happening on one day when it’s another. I’ve been confused about exhibit dates too, and then I second-guess myself and have to check everything again. And I volunteer—well, I try to keep track of who I’ve called and who I haven’t—and lately I’ll realize I’m not sure whether I already called someone or just meant to call them. It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad exactly. I mean, I’ve been frustrated and worried because I don’t like feeling so mixed up, and it gets to me when I make mistakes with things I used to handle just fine. But “sad or down”... I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.

I do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.

I suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.

At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.

I’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.

So yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.

I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.

Some mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. I haven’t really been comparing it hour by hour, if that makes sense.

So… I don’t think I can say yes, that mornings are definitely the worst. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I’ve just been feeling like my head isn’t working the way it should. It’s like there’s a fog over everything, and I keep mixing up simple things that I normally would have no trouble keeping straight. \n\nFor example, I’ll get appointment times wrong, or I’ll think something is happening on one day when it’s another. I’ve been confused about exhibit dates too, and then I second-guess myself and have to check everything again. And I volunteer—well, I try to keep track of who I’ve called and who I haven’t—and lately I’ll realize I’m not sure whether I already called someone or just meant to call them. It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad exactly. I mean, I’ve been frustrated and worried because I don’t like feeling so mixed up, and it gets to me when I make mistakes with things I used to handle just fine. But “sad or down”... I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up in the morning feeling worse than you do the rest of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. I haven’t really been comparing it hour by hour, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… I don’t think I can say yes, that mornings are definitely the worst. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up in the morning feeling worse than you do the rest of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. I haven’t really been comparing it hour by hour, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… I don’t think I can say yes, that mornings are definitely the worst. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you lost a significant amount of weight, such as more than 5% of your body weight, over the past few months?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 79 / not_in_workforce / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Weight_Appetite_Change\nmanifestation: \"Most days I pick at toast or soup because food doesn't sound good, and my skirts have gotten loose without me trying to lose weight.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes, I think I have, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number or percentage.

What I’ve noticed more is how my clothes fit. A couple of my skirts that used to sit properly at the waist have gotten loose, enough that I find myself tugging at them or thinking I should maybe pin them. And I haven’t been trying to lose weight. That’s the part that makes me notice it.

Food just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll have a little soup because it’s easy and I know I should eat something. But I’m not sitting down to proper meals the way I used to. It’s more like picking at things. So yes… I don’t know if it’s over 5%, exactly, but I do think I’ve lost weight over these past few months without meaning to.

It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I was up early moving supplies, rewriting lists, calling people, and rearranging rooms long after everyone else was worn out.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, such as more than 5% of your body weight, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number or percentage.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed more is how my clothes fit. A couple of my skirts that used to sit properly at the waist have gotten loose, enough that I find myself tugging at them or thinking I should maybe pin them. And I haven’t been trying to lose weight. That’s the part that makes me notice it.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll have a little soup because it’s easy and I know I should eat something. But I’m not sitting down to proper meals the way I used to. It’s more like picking at things.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, very much so. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself.

I used to love my little watercolors. Nothing fancy, you know, just sitting at the table and painting flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside. I still have the paints there, and the paper, and I’ll look at them and think, “I should do that, it used to make me feel peaceful,” but then I just… don’t. I can’t seem to care enough to start.

And my tomato plants too. I used to fuss over them, check the leaves, see if they needed water, get excited when the little green ones started coming in. Now they’re just sitting there, and I know I should care, but it feels like it’s happening behind glass or something.

Even line dancing — I used to enjoy that, the music and seeing people, remembering the steps. Now the thought of going just feels like too much, and not in a fun way. And I used to sit and watch the cardinals out back. I really loved that. Now I see them and it’s like… well, there’s a bird. That’s all. It doesn’t lift me the way it used to.

So yes, the things I used to love are still there, but I can’t make myself feel much for them right now.

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I came in because I’ve just been feeling like my head isn’t working the way it should. It’s like there’s a fog over everything, and I keep mixing up simple things that I normally would have no trouble keeping straight. \n\nFor example, I’ll get appointment times wrong, or I’ll think something is happening on one day when it’s another. I’ve been confused about exhibit dates too, and then I second-guess myself and have to check everything again. And I volunteer—well, I try to keep track of who I’ve called and who I haven’t—and lately I’ll realize I’m not sure whether I already called someone or just meant to call them. It’s embarrassing, really, because these aren’t complicated things. It just feels like my thinking has slowed down or gotten muddled somehow.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d call it sad exactly. I mean, I’ve been frustrated and worried because I don’t like feeling so mixed up, and it gets to me when I make mistakes with things I used to handle just fine. But “sad or down”... I don’t know. I haven’t really stopped to name it that way. It’s more like I feel bothered by all this and a little embarrassed, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious lately?\n\nPatient: Anxious… I don’t know. Maybe a little, in the sense that I get uneasy when I realize I’ve mixed something up, because then I start thinking, “Well, what else did I get wrong?” But I’m not sure if that’s what you mean by anxious.\n\nI wouldn’t say I’ve been sitting around with panic or anything like that. It’s more that I feel unsettled because I don’t trust myself the way I used to with ordinary things. Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up in the morning feeling worse than you do the rest of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. So yes… I don’t know if it’s over 5%, exactly, but I do think I’ve lost weight over these past few months without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, very much so. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up in the morning feeling worse than you do the rest of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. I haven’t really been comparing it hour by hour, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… I don’t think I can say yes, that mornings are definitely the worst. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, such as more than 5% of your body weight, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number or percentage.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed more is how my clothes fit. A couple of my skirts that used to sit properly at the waist have gotten loose, enough that I find myself tugging at them or thinking I should maybe pin them. And I haven’t been trying to lose weight. That’s the part that makes me notice it.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll have a little soup because it’s easy and I know I should eat something. But I’m not sitting down to proper meals the way I used to. It’s more like picking at things. So yes… I don’t know if it’s over 5%, exactly, but I do think I’ve lost weight over these past few months without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, very much so. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself.\n\nI used to love my little watercolors. Nothing fancy, you know, just sitting at the table and painting flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside. I still have the paints there, and the paper, and I’ll look at them and think, “I should do that, it used to make me feel peaceful,” but then I just… don’t. I can’t seem to care enough to start.\n\nAnd my tomato plants too. I used to fuss over them, check the leaves, see if they needed water, get excited when the little green ones started coming in. Now they’re just sitting there, and I know I should care, but it feels like it’s happening behind glass or something.\n\nEven line dancing — I used to enjoy that, the music and seeing people, remembering the steps. Now the thought of going just feels like too much, and not in a fun way. And I used to sit and watch the cardinals out back. I really loved that. Now I see them and it’s like… well, there’s a bird. That’s all. It doesn’t lift me the way it used to.\n\nSo yes, the things I used to love are still there, but I can’t make myself feel much for them right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.   },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

I don’t know that I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That feels like such a strong word, and I’m not sure I walk around thinking that in a clear sentence, like “I’m worthless.” It’s more muddled than that.

I do sometimes feel like I’m not doing what I ought to be doing, if that makes sense. Like I’ll look around at things I haven’t kept up with, or I’ll think about how I used to be more on top of things, more useful maybe, and then I feel disappointed in myself. But I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling worthless.

As far as letting people down… I suppose I worry about being a burden, or not being the version of myself people expect. But again, it’s hard for me to say how much of that is just ordinary worry and how much is something more. I don’t sit there with a list of people I’ve failed, but I do get that heavy feeling sometimes, like I should be doing better than I am.

I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that.
