Like if I’m checking times or trying to remember whether I made a call, I can get worked up about it, but I think that’s because of the confusion, not just anxiety out of nowhere.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been enjoying the things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. I mean, I wouldn’t have said it that way right away, but now that you ask, yes. Things I used to really enjoy just don’t pull me in anymore.\n\nI used to love doing my watercolors—nothing fancy, just little flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside—and now the paints are just sitting there. I’ll look at them and think, “I should get those out,” but I don’t. Same with my tomato plants. I used to fuss over them, check them every morning, get excited when there was a new little green one coming in. Now I see them out there and it’s like… I know I should care, but I can’t quite make myself.\n\nAnd I used to go line dancing, which I enjoyed more than I ever thought I would at my age. But lately even that feels like too much, or like it belongs to someone else. Even watching the cardinals out back—there are a pair that come around, and I used to sit and watch them with my coffee—now I’ll notice them, but the feeling isn’t there the way it used to be. It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I just look at food and don’t feel much interest in it, and then I don’t end up eating very much.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been feeling hopeless about the future?\n\nPatient: Hopeless… I’m not sure I’d use that word exactly. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. But hopeless… I don’t know. I wouldn’t want to say yes if that means something very strong. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like everything just sits there, and I can’t quite make myself care.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling guilty about anything lately?\n\nPatient: Guilty… hmm. I don’t know. I don’t think I’ve been feeling guilty in any big, constant way, if that’s what you mean.\n\nI do sometimes think, “I should be doing more,” or “I’m not keeping up with things the way I ought to,” but I’m not sure I’d call that guilt exactly. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. But I haven’t been sitting around blaming myself for some particular thing, or feeling like I’ve done something terribly wrong.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think guilt has been a main thing lately. At least not that I’ve noticed.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Sad most of the day… nearly every day… I’m not sure. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Sometimes it’s more quiet than that. But nearly every day, most of the day… I don’t know. I haven’t been keeping track closely enough to answer that with confidence.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been experiencing these low feelings for more than two weeks?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, it’s definitely been more than two weeks. I wish I could say it was just a rough patch for a few days, but it’s been longer than that.\n\nI’d say this low, slowed-down part of me has been going on for about three months now. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up in the morning feeling worse than you do the rest of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. I haven’t really been comparing it hour by hour, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… I don’t think I can say yes, that mornings are definitely the worst. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, such as more than 5% of your body weight, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number or percentage.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed more is how my clothes fit. A couple of my skirts that used to sit properly at the waist have gotten loose, enough that I find myself tugging at them or thinking I should maybe pin them. And I haven’t been trying to lose weight. That’s the part that makes me notice it.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll have a little soup because it’s easy and I know I should eat something. But I’m not sitting down to proper meals the way I used to. It’s more like picking at things. So yes… I don’t know if it’s over 5%, exactly, but I do think I’ve lost weight over these past few months without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, very much so. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself.\n\nI used to love my little watercolors. Nothing fancy, you know, just sitting at the table and painting flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside. I still have the paints there, and the paper, and I’ll look at them and think, “I should do that, it used to make me feel peaceful,” but then I just… don’t. I can’t seem to care enough to start.\n\nAnd my tomato plants too. I used to fuss over them, check the leaves, see if they needed water, get excited when the little green ones started coming in. Now they’re just sitting there, and I know I should care, but it feels like it’s happening behind glass or something.\n\nEven line dancing — I used to enjoy that, the music and seeing people, remembering the steps. Now the thought of going just feels like too much, and not in a fun way. And I used to sit and watch the cardinals out back. I really loved that. Now I see them and it’s like… well, there’s a bird. That’s all. It doesn’t lift me the way it used to.\n\nSo yes, the things I used to love are still there, but I can’t make myself feel much for them right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless or that you've let yourself or others down?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That feels like such a strong word, and I’m not sure I walk around thinking that in a clear sentence, like “I’m worthless.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI do sometimes feel like I’m not doing what I ought to be doing, if that makes sense. Like I’ll look around at things I haven’t kept up with, or I’ll think about how I used to be more on top of things, more useful maybe, and then I feel disappointed in myself. But I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling worthless.\n\nAs far as letting people down… I suppose I worry about being a burden, or not being the version of myself people expect. But again, it’s hard for me to say how much of that is just ordinary worry and how much is something more. I don’t sit there with a list of people I’ve failed, but I do get that heavy feeling sometimes, like I should be doing better than I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I don’t know if I would call it “sad” most days, exactly. Sometimes it feels less like crying sadness and more like everything is sort of dimmed down, if that makes sense.\n\nThere are days where I do feel low, yes, but I’m not always sitting there thinking, “I’m sad.” It’s more that I wake up and the day already feels heavy, or flat, and I have to sort of push myself through it. But then I wonder if that counts as sadness, or if sadness is supposed to mean you’re actively upset or tearful.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I feel down a lot of the time, but I’m hesitant to say “sad most days” because it doesn’t always have that clear emotional shape to it. It’s just there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably right. I don’t know if it’s every single minute of the day, but most of the time, the pleasure just isn’t there the way it used to be.\n\nLike I was saying, I’ll look at my watercolors, and I remember that I used to enjoy setting everything out just so — the little cup of water, the brushes, picking a color — and now they just sit there. My tomato plants too, they’re out there needing attention, and I used to get such a little thrill from seeing them grow. Now I notice them, but I don’t really feel anything.\n\nLine dancing used to be fun for me, not just the dancing but the whole thing, the music and the people. And watching the cardinals out back — that used to make me feel peaceful. Now it’s like my mind knows those things are supposed to matter to me, but the feeling doesn’t come with it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say I’m unable to feel much pleasure most of the time. It’s not that the things disappeared. It’s more like I can’t reach the part of me that used to enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I didn’t notice it at first, or I thought maybe I was just being careful because I’m older, you know, but my husband has pointed it out more than once. He says I move like I’m wading through mud now, which isn’t very flattering, but I know what he means.\n\nEven little things take me so long. If the phone rings, by the time I get myself up and over to it, it feels like it’s already been ringing forever. And sorting the mail — that used to be nothing, just stand there and flip through it — now I’ll sit with it in my lap and it takes me ages to separate what’s important from what’s junk.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to go slowly. It’s like my body just doesn’t get started the way it used to, and every movement has extra weight to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact percentage, because I haven’t been weighing myself carefully every day or anything like that. But I have lost some weight, yes, and it wasn’t on purpose.\n\nMost days I just sort of pick at food. I’ll make toast and eat maybe part of it, or have a little soup because it feels easy, but food doesn’t really sound good to me. It’s not that I’m trying to diet — at my age I’m certainly not trying to get thinner — it’s more like I look at a plate and just can’t get interested in it.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my skirts are looser around the waist. Things don’t sit the same way on me. So whether it’s more than 5%, I’m not sure, but there’s definitely been weight coming off without me meaning for it to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It’s more than mild, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do anything at all, because some days I still manage bits and pieces, but it’s definitely interfering with my life in a way I’m embarrassed to admit.

I’ve missed doctor visits, which is not like me. I used to keep all that straight — the little appointment cards, the calendar on the refrigerator, making sure I knew which office I was going to. Lately I get mixed up, or I think I’ve written something down and then I can’t find it, or I just don’t follow through the way I used to.

And the senior center schedule… oh, I made such a mess of that. I got the days confused and the times confused, and then I was calling people back and not remembering who I’d already talked to. My husband finally had to step in and help me with the calls and the calendar, because it had gotten so tangled up.

He’s also been helping with repair decisions around the house, things I used to just handle. If something needed fixing, I’d call someone, compare what they said, pick a time. Now I just sit with it too long or get overwhelmed by the whole thing. So no, I wouldn’t call it mild. It’s affecting ordinary things I used to manage myself.

It’s not that I hate those things. They just sort of sit there, and I can’t seem to care about them like I normally would.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost any weight recently?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have. I haven’t been trying to, but my skirts have gotten loose around the waist, the ones that usually fit just right. At first I thought maybe the elastic was giving out or I’d stretched them somehow, but then it was more than one skirt, so… yes, I suppose I have lost some weight.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll heat up soup and sort of pick at it more than anything. It’s not that I’m making a plan to diet or anything like that. I mean, I do have moments where I look ahead and it feels sort of cloudy, like I can’t picture things very clearly, but I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling hopeless.\n\nI haven’t really been sitting there thinking, “There’s no future,” if that’s what you mean. It’s more that I don’t seem to think very far ahead lately. I’ll think about today, or maybe tomorrow, and then beyond that it just sort of feels hard to get my mind around. I guess I’d say I feel unsure more than hopeless.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to enjoy, or is it more that you're just not feeling up to doing them lately?\n\nPatient: I think it’s both, but if I’m being honest, it’s more that the interest just isn’t there. It’s not only that I’m too tired or that it’s too much trouble, though sometimes I do tell myself that. It’s that the things themselves don’t seem to call to me the way they used to.\n\nLike my watercolors… I used to really enjoy setting everything out, picking the colors, just losing myself in painting little flowers or birds. Now I see the paints sitting there and I think, “I ought to do that,” but there’s no pull. My tomato plants are the same way. I used to fuss over them and check on them every morning, and now they’re just out there and I know I should care, but I don’t really feel it.\n\nAnd line dancing—I used to look forward to that. I’d think about what blouse I might wear, who I’d see, that sort of thing. Now it just feels like something I used to do, almost like it belongs to a different version of me. Even the cardinals out back, I still notice them, but I don’t get that little lift from watching them like I used to.\n\nSo yes, I guess I have lost interest. It’s not that I dislike any of it. It’s more like a nagging feeling, maybe, or being disappointed in myself a little. I don’t know that I would put it quite that neatly.\n\nI do have spells where I feel low, or kind of heavy inside, but if you ask me whether it’s sadness from morning to night, day after day, I’d have a hard time saying yes to that. Some days I wake up and I don’t feel much of anything at first, and then later something might come over me. Other times I’m just sort of flat, not exactly crying or feeling heartbroken, but not right either.\n\nI suppose I’m hesitating because “sad” sounds very specific to me, like grieving or weeping, and that isn’t always how it feels. Maybe not every single minute in exactly the same way, but it’s been there, kind of hanging over things. Long enough that I’ve stopped thinking, “Oh, I’ll feel better next week,” because several weeks have come and gone and I still feel like I’m moving through mud a lot of the time.\n\nSo yes, more than two weeks. Closer to three months, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you often wake up in the morning feeling worse than you do the rest of the day?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’ve noticed a clear pattern like that. Mornings can be hard in their own way, but I don’t know that I consistently feel worse then than I do later in the day.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and think, “Oh, here we are again,” and it takes me a while to get myself going, but there are also times when the afternoon or evening feels just as difficult, or it sort of changes without much rhyme or reason. I haven’t really been comparing it hour by hour, if that makes sense.\n\nSo… I don’t think I can say yes, that mornings are definitely the worst. I just haven’t noticed it clearly enough.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight, such as more than 5% of your body weight, over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think I have, though I haven’t been weighing myself regularly, so I can’t give you an exact number or percentage.\n\nWhat I’ve noticed more is how my clothes fit. A couple of my skirts that used to sit properly at the waist have gotten loose, enough that I find myself tugging at them or thinking I should maybe pin them. And I haven’t been trying to lose weight. That’s the part that makes me notice it.\n\nFood just hasn’t sounded good most days. I’ll make toast and then only eat part of it, or I’ll have a little soup because it’s easy and I know I should eat something. But I’m not sitting down to proper meals the way I used to. It’s more like picking at things. So yes… I don’t know if it’s over 5%, exactly, but I do think I’ve lost weight over these past few months without meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you no longer enjoy activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: Yes… yes, very much so. That’s actually one of the things that makes me feel most unlike myself.\n\nI used to love my little watercolors. Nothing fancy, you know, just sitting at the table and painting flowers or birds or whatever I saw outside. I still have the paints there, and the paper, and I’ll look at them and think, “I should do that, it used to make me feel peaceful,” but then I just… don’t. I can’t seem to care enough to start.\n\nAnd my tomato plants too. I used to fuss over them, check the leaves, see if they needed water, get excited when the little green ones started coming in. Now they’re just sitting there, and I know I should care, but it feels like it’s happening behind glass or something.\n\nEven line dancing — I used to enjoy that, the music and seeing people, remembering the steps. Now the thought of going just feels like too much, and not in a fun way. And I used to sit and watch the cardinals out back. I really loved that. Now I see them and it’s like… well, there’s a bird. That’s all. It doesn’t lift me the way it used to.\n\nSo yes, the things I used to love are still there, but I can’t make myself feel much for them right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel like you're worthless or that you've let yourself or others down?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that I’d say “worthless,” exactly. That feels like such a strong word, and I’m not sure I walk around thinking that in a clear sentence, like “I’m worthless.” It’s more muddled than that.\n\nI do sometimes feel like I’m not doing what I ought to be doing, if that makes sense. Like I’ll look around at things I haven’t kept up with, or I’ll think about how I used to be more on top of things, more useful maybe, and then I feel disappointed in myself. But I don’t know if that’s the same as feeling worthless.\n\nAs far as letting people down… I suppose I worry about being a burden, or not being the version of myself people expect. But again, it’s hard for me to say how much of that is just ordinary worry and how much is something more. I don’t sit there with a list of people I’ve failed, but I do get that heavy feeling sometimes, like I should be doing better than I am.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most days?\n\nPatient: I’m not completely sure how to answer that. I don’t know if I would call it “sad” most days, exactly. Sometimes it feels less like crying sadness and more like everything is sort of dimmed down, if that makes sense.\n\nThere are days where I do feel low, yes, but I’m not always sitting there thinking, “I’m sad.” It’s more that I wake up and the day already feels heavy, or flat, and I have to sort of push myself through it. But then I wonder if that counts as sadness, or if sadness is supposed to mean you’re actively upset or tearful.\n\nSo… I guess I’d say I feel down a lot of the time, but I’m hesitant to say “sad most days” because it doesn’t always have that clear emotional shape to it. It’s just there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think that’s probably right. I don’t know if it’s every single minute of the day, but most of the time, the pleasure just isn’t there the way it used to be.\n\nLike I was saying, I’ll look at my watercolors, and I remember that I used to enjoy setting everything out just so — the little cup of water, the brushes, picking a color — and now they just sit there. My tomato plants too, they’re out there needing attention, and I used to get such a little thrill from seeing them grow. Now I notice them, but I don’t really feel anything.\n\nLine dancing used to be fun for me, not just the dancing but the whole thing, the music and the people. And watching the cardinals out back — that used to make me feel peaceful. Now it’s like my mind knows those things are supposed to matter to me, but the feeling doesn’t come with it.\n\nSo yes, I’d say I’m unable to feel much pleasure most of the time. It’s not that the things disappeared. It’s more like I can’t reach the part of me that used to enjoy them.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: Yes, I think so. I didn’t notice it at first, or I thought maybe I was just being careful because I’m older, you know, but my husband has pointed it out more than once. He says I move like I’m wading through mud now, which isn’t very flattering, but I know what he means.\n\nEven little things take me so long. If the phone rings, by the time I get myself up and over to it, it feels like it’s already been ringing forever. And sorting the mail — that used to be nothing, just stand there and flip through it — now I’ll sit with it in my lap and it takes me ages to separate what’s important from what’s junk.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to go slowly. It’s like my body just doesn’t get started the way it used to, and every movement has extra weight to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact percentage, because I haven’t been weighing myself carefully every day or anything like that. But I have lost some weight, yes, and it wasn’t on purpose.\n\nMost days I just sort of pick at food. I’ll make toast and eat maybe part of it, or have a little soup because it feels easy, but food doesn’t really sound good to me. It’s not that I’m trying to diet — at my age I’m certainly not trying to get thinner — it’s more like I look at a plate and just can’t get interested in it.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my skirts are looser around the waist. Things don’t sit the same way on me. So whether it’s more than 5%, I’m not sure, but there’s definitely been weight coming off without me meaning for it to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your depression is mild, or is it interfering more significantly with your daily life?\n\nPatient: It’s more than mild, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do anything at all, because some days I still manage bits and pieces, but it’s definitely interfering with my life in a way I’m embarrassed to admit.\n\nI’ve missed doctor visits, which is not like me. I used to keep all that straight — the little appointment cards, the calendar on the refrigerator, making sure I knew which office I was going to. Lately I get mixed up, or I think I’ve written something down and then I can’t find it, or I just don’t follow through the way I used to.\n\nAnd the senior center schedule… oh, I made such a mess of that. I got the days confused and the times confused, and then I was calling people back and not remembering who I’d already talked to. My husband finally had to step in and help me with the calls and the calendar, because it had gotten so tangled up.\n\nHe’s also been helping with repair decisions around the house, things I used to just handle. If something needed fixing, I’d call someone, compare what they said, pick a time. Now I just sit with it too long or get overwhelmed by the whole thing. So no, I wouldn’t call it mild. It’s affecting ordinary things I used to manage myself.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

And sorting the mail — that used to be nothing, just stand there and flip through it — now I’ll sit with it in my lap and it takes me ages to separate what’s important from what’s junk.\n\nIt’s not that I’m trying to go slowly. It’s like my body just doesn’t get started the way it used to, and every movement has extra weight to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost more than 5% of your body weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: I don’t know the exact percentage, because I haven’t been weighing myself carefully every day or anything like that. But I have lost some weight, yes, and it wasn’t on purpose.\n\nMost days I just sort of pick at food. I’ll make toast and eat maybe part of it, or have a little soup because it feels easy, but food doesn’t really sound good to me. It’s not that I’m trying to diet — at my age I’m certainly not trying to get thinner — it’s more like I look at a plate and just can’t get interested in it.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my skirts are looser around the waist. Things don’t sit the same way on me. So whether it’s more than 5%, I’m not sure, but there’s definitely been weight coming off without me meaning for it to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your depression is mild, or is it interfering more significantly with your daily life?\n\nPatient: It’s more than mild, I think. I don’t want to make it sound like I can’t do anything at all, because some days I still manage bits and pieces, but it’s definitely interfering with my life in a way I’m embarrassed to admit.\n\nI’ve missed doctor visits, which is not like me. I used to keep all that straight — the little appointment cards, the calendar on the refrigerator, making sure I knew which office I was going to. Lately I get mixed up, or I think I’ve written something down and then I can’t find it, or I just don’t follow through the way I used to.\n\nAnd the senior center schedule… oh, I made such a mess of that. I got the days confused and the times confused, and then I was calling people back and not remembering who I’d already talked to. My husband finally had to step in and help me with the calls and the calendar, because it had gotten so tangled up.\n\nHe’s also been helping with repair decisions around the house, things I used to just handle. If something needed fixing, I’d call someone, compare what they said, pick a time. Now I just sit with it too long or get overwhelmed by the whole thing. So no, I wouldn’t call it mild. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yes… yes, I’d say it does, most of the time. I wish I could say it just comes and goes and I can work around it, but lately it keeps getting into ordinary things.

Like I’ve missed doctor visits, which really bothers me because I used to be very careful about those. I’d keep the cards, write it on the calendar, know what time I had to leave. Now I get turned around or I don’t follow through, and then suddenly I’ve missed something.

And with the senior center schedule, I made such a tangle of it — the days, the calls, who I’d already spoken to. It got embarrassing. My husband had to step in and help me sort out the phone calls and the calendar, and he’s been helping with decisions about repairs around the house too. Those were things I used to handle myself without making such a production of it.

So yes, it’s not just that I feel low and keep it to myself. It’s spilling over into the daily things.
